ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00058_d7e2515	Mr President. High house! I would like to thank Mr Tancsits for making the issue of employment policy a central issue the very day after the budget speech, because for us this is indeed one thing: yesterday's presentation of the path that should lead us away from debt policy, and today's commitment to the fact that this cannot be done at the price of higher unemployment. For the first time, we want to combine a debt-free fiscal policy with a full employment policy. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Some will remember that Bruno Kreisky once said that he was more indebted than a higher number of unemployed people. We know the result: In the end, we both had high unemployment rates and a debt mountain for Austrian conditions. [[Abg. Edler: What about other countries?]] We want to do it quite differently. My thesis is: We don't want debt, but we really want to do "work for all" for that! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Clearly, this has not been done with the confession alone; one must also show ways in which we get there. [[Abg. Edler: Thatcherism!]] I think just the last months, last year has proved: It's okay! It is difficult, it requires creativity, it requires above all new approaches in labour market policy, in employment policy, but it works! First: An important recipe is more competition, liberalisation, breaking up certain protected structures, deregulation, privatisation. A very nice example of a successful privatized company, which today offers more jobs than ever before, has already been mentioned by Mr Tancsits. More competition, more liberalisation, of course, means cost advantages for businesses. If, for example, electricity markets are fully opened from October onwards and therefore an important raw material, important energy, for businesses, for labour markets, this is good for jobs. We have implemented this – just as it is with Telekom, as with the gas market. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] It is also apparent that the liberalisation efforts in the trade order of the former Minister of Economic Affairs Farnleiter are now bearing fruit. It has provided virtually general forbearance if a young businessman does not yet have all the conditions. It has introduced a significant simplification, part-time business – and see, last year we had 24 000 new business start-ups. This is 10 percent more than in the previous year, twice as many as in the mid-1990s. That is the way to really help the middle class, especially the young entrepreneurs! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The second way is to: Priority for education, research and information technology. Minister for Education Elisabeth Gehrer has already made important points here. Today we have 20 000 graduates per year at HTLs and HAKs. I'm allowed They shall inform: In 2002, we will have more than 2,000 graduates in these middle and higher schools. [[Abg. Brosz: 2 200 teachers less!]] Today we have 850 graduates in this field at the universities. Liesl Gehrer has achieved that it will be 4,000 graduates in the university field in 2003. 40 new courses and classes will be introduced immediately. This is a very important point that will significantly increase the quality of Austria's location in the future. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] In addition, 7 billion schilling for research and development, in the reform dialogue now with the Technology and Research Council in preparation, in full concrete implementation, the reform of universities and also more presence for teachers in schools. [[Abg. Shaching: 2 000 teachers less – what about it?]] [[Abg. Shasching: Every year but less teachers!]] I say quite frankly to you: I do not understand if some standing representatives – thank God by far not all – so strongly oppose these needs, in the interests of young people and their quality of training. We won't accept that, dear friends! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The third priority, of course, is a modern administration: a simpler modern plant law, companies should really be able to do something and not be inhibited by bureaucracy, eGovernment, a better investment, settlement policy – the Austrian Business Agency, which some socialist ministers once wanted to starve, flourishes today and has brought a lot of new businesses to Austria. You have to thank people there very much, because they were able to put a real accent here last year. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Above all, it has been achieved that the unemployment administration – as it used to be called – is now really becoming a labour market service. I myself have tried to do this as Minister of Economic Affairs with the former Minister of Social Affairs Hesoun, it has often been disabled and inhibited. Today this finally begins to work well in the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Labour, and the results can be seen: [[Abg. Oellinger: You're making an unemployment management out of it again! They state again...!]] We had some 28 000 new jobs last year. We have 27 000 additional employees, especially in women, which is women's policy, not rhetoric, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] We have 7 000 unemployed people over the age of 50 – a decrease of over 12 percent! – and above all for the unemployed young people a 13 per cent decrease. With this, Austria has the lowest unemployment rate in young people across the EU – we can truly be proud of that! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] [[Abg. Edler: We had them before!]] A sentence on the long-term unemployed: We have a significant decline in those who are unemployed for six months, by 27 per cent, even one of almost 40 per cent for those who are unemployed over 12 months. Now I say something about it – I say that especially in the direction of my friend Andreas Khol, who fought for this idea and had to put many hits in: This project is primarily responsible for the fact that we have such a strong reduction of almost 40 percent for the long-term unemployed. At this point I would like to thank all the communities that exploit this. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] [[Abg. Oellinger: Please, Minister, correct this! Correct the chancellor!]] Citizens' money is mainly handled through the communities in connection with labour market services. Dr. Kostelka: We have a campaign now! Today and now you are running election campaign!]] Listen and marvel! The municipality of Vienna has almost not participated. In the education system – and this was also explained by Mr Van der Bellen – it is the case that an increase alone does not mean that something is being expanded. In principle, as in other areas, an increase serves to maintain the existence at all. If you also add to the structural effect that has been mentioned what to pay for salary payments, you will be able to assume that an increase in the order of 3 to 4 percent per year would have to be done in order to maintain the status quo at all. This is not too difficult to calculate at about 100 billion schillings: 3 to 4 billion shillings annually, which for three years represents at least 9 billion shillings that would be necessary to maintain only the system. So if you're talking about a big educational offensive here, you have to look at the numbers more closely. If you look at the functional area, you can already see that a massive reduction is actually taking place in the field of education. However, if you look a little more closely, you will find that this apparent increase from 1999 to 2002 is primarily and largely due to the fact that the budget has increased from 1999 to 2000. – No wonder you consider how late this budget was created at the time; there were relatively few ways of saving. At that time it was already 107 billion schilling. From 2000 to 2002, there was an increase of 3 billion in two years. ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00058_d7e12714	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Today's budget shows some very positive, notable aspects from the economic point of view. After all, it is the third budget within a year that is being presented here, and if we remember that a year ago we got the figures to draw up the budget for the year 2000, then this is a clear progress and speaks for the work also in the ministry. It also speaks for the budgetary discipline of the respective departments, and it is certainly positive to highlight that there has been consistent work here because this is the prerequisite for a continuous line. This is – this has been mentioned several times today – the first financial year after 30 years, in which a general government zero deficit could again be achieved. It is particularly gratifying that the EU also accepts and supports this path of consolidation as a whole. It is perhaps the most pleasing thing, however, that we have achieved a turnaround, a turnaround, in that we no longer align our budget policy according to expenditure, but go the opposite way: We are submitting a budget that is geared towards revenue. This is nothing but the abandonment of the old debt policy. Ladies and gentlemen! In this context, I would also like to make a slightly more differentiated presentation by the Socialists, because at the end of the day, whether a federal government is politically oriented, even in all the other countries of Europe, there is no other way of fiscal consolidation than that which is going on here today and which has gone over the last few months. It may be understandable if, in his opinion, a finance minister who believed that he was doing good budgetary policy is subjectively disappointed if he gets something else confirmed by the EU, namely that the objective conditions are different. However, one should be oriented to the actual figures, and therefore a differentiated discussion would be desirable here. I would also like to point out that research and development are actually the basis for the future and that there is a nice dot of 10 billion schilling here. It should be even higher, but it is a good point of approach. Ladies and gentlemen! However, from the point of view of the economy, it is also to be asked, and Brussels also asks: What's the budget worth? What is it worth in 2003? – In this respect, we believe that we need to be very careful that this is a transitory stage and that the real decision will not be taken until 2003. Why? Because the central question is the sustainability of the budget. This was also addressed by Günter Stummvoll today. The economy is booming! The labour market situation is positive, and exports have achieved record values. But the tax rate has also reached a record value, and 44.7 percent are certainly an opportunity to counter it accordingly. There are, in fact, two reasons to counter: First of all, the EU's call for sustainability. The EU says that there is an opportunity to counter demographic development, namely the increase in the employment rate. What do we mean by increasing the employment rate? Quite simply: that the wage tax does not rise as it does in this budget, by 15 billion schillings, that the cost does not rise to this extent, as here by at least 3 billion schillings, but that workers, but also entrepreneurs, remain in their field. For this reason, in order to ensure the increase in the employment rate, a reduction in additional labour costs, but also a reduction in corporate taxes in 2003 is a very important matter. But there is also a second reason why we need to ensure sustainability, and that is the competitive situation in Europe. All other countries that already have budget surpluses – eight in total – and two others are on their way to do so – will have tax reforms until 2006 and will reduce taxes, partly up to 2% of the respective gross national product. Ladies and gentlemen! This will certainly be the test for us, for the government. There must also be an adjustment, a tax adjustment for competition reasons, otherwise investors will not invest in Austria or the Austrian will invest abroad in times of the euro. It is therefore important to create the basis now for a sustainable tax reform in the area of administrative reform. We have a sentence from the point of view of the economy: We want our money back! Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00058_d7e13193	Mr President. Minister! Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! I would like to comment on the points raised in the urgent request as follows: First: In the Mazal report on social security, it was clearly stated that, in comparison to the tax liability for invalidity pensions and the tax liability for private accident insurance pensions, pensions from the statutory accident insurance are not subject to tax liability. Parliament's decisions wanted to eliminate these differences in taxation. A tax on accident pensions was introduced with the EStG in 1988, but was abolished by the Constitutional Court because the tax rate was linked to the extent of the reduction in employability. In the view of the VfGH, this was not justified because a tax on "percentage of this disability" with a minimum limit of 20 percent was considered to be inadequate and therefore inappropriate criterion for the delimitation of taxable incomes of tax-free compensation for other disadvantages. The second point: With effect from 1. In January 2001, the unemployment insurance system was converted from an impenetrable and complex system with 147 wage classes to a manageable net replacement rate system. Unemployment benefit is now paid at a rate of 55 percent of the previous net basis of calculation. This is not a deterioration, as the average level according to the wage-class system was 54.9%. At the same time, a unemployment benefit has been introduced to ensure that at least 60 percent of those whose unemployment benefit is below the compensatory allowance rate. This first social security will in particular benefit women. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The measures in the field of family allowances must be seen in their entirety, because this is particularly true to the idea of social security if the consideration of family care obligations in all social security systems is identical. ==References== In January 2001, a reduction in the amount of family allowance in unemployment insurance was removed, but at the same time, the previously planned accounting of partner income was abolished. This means, in particular, that women who have become unemployed now receive a family allowance from the experience of the past. If worsening in unemployment insurance is to be understood that with 1. In January 2001, no dynamisation of emergency aid was carried out, it should be pointed out that this was not done in the years 1996 to 1999. To point 3: The Federal Government has taken decisions of the Council of Ministers of the 19th century. In September 2000 it was clearly declared a quality and performance offensive at the Austrian universities. The "Modern Study and Research" project therefore includes the entire complex of necessary and sustainable further developments in the university area. Not study contributions alone, but a modern and performance-oriented service law, the extension of university autonomy to full legal capacity and the resulting strengthening of the Austrian scientific location are the goal. Education is the raw material for our country. [[Abg. Edler: What are you doing?]] That's why I'm once again stressing: No one will be able to study for financial reasons. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The compensatory and accompanying measures for the study contributions from the coming winter semester are therefore: Reimbursement of study contributions for all students of study aid. This means that approximately 30 000 persons affected will receive an additional 10 000 S each year. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] The increase and change to an annual view of the amount of earnings for students. Therefore, 12 500 people will be more involved in the aid system. A further 3,000 persons will be awarded a benefit grant through the re-construction of the service grants and the threefolding of the funds provided for them. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Finally, there will be interest-privileged loans to finance the study contributions. The state already supports around 40 percent of all students – I emphasize: all – with the family support and the study aid together. As a result of the expansions of the relationship, it will be around 45 percent. This is a top value in Europe. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] The revenue from the study contributions is used to improve the teaching situation at universities. In July 2001 the 500 million Schilling and in 2002 1 billion Schilling were held. If you want a good training for our children, you have to confess to this sustainable quality improvement. Ladies and gentlemen! We are committed to a policy that clearly puts figures and facts on the table. No re-indebtedness. The improvement of the university educational situation is not a self-interest, but must be borne by the consciousness and responsibility for our children. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] To point 4: Instead of non-contributory co-insurance, an additional contribution in health insurance is intended for the co-insurance of certain members. The additional contribution must be paid: first, for spouses, second for partners, third for parents, electoral, step-and-step parents, children, election, step-and-care children, the grandson or siblings of the insured person, who is considered to be household members, for the duration of their co-insurance. The additional contribution shall not be payable: for co-insurance children, elective children, step-and-care children or grandchildren or spouses, life-mates who have currently cared for or at some point have taken care of a child, but also if the co-insuranced family receives care benefit at least at the level of level 4, the co-insuranced family is the insured person who receives at least the care allowance at level 4; if there is a social need for protection under the guidelines of the main association – this is especially the case if the monthly net income of the insured person does not exceed the compensation allowance rate for married couples – during the reference of sickness, weekly allowance, care allowance, unemployment benefit or emergency aid. This measure is to be referred to as socially sound, in so far as those persons who were excluded from work due to child care obligations and close relatives were unable to acquire their own insurance, continue to be insured without contributions. – Thank you for your attention.

In 1992, 22 points were drawn up, which were commissioned by the Minister of Health at the time. But it wasn't done. In 1997, an LKF system was introduced, which was carried out half-heartedly, inconsistency, with social cold and which in no way brought the success it was supposed to bring. Let us not forget that the accident victim often suffers for years from the consequences of the accident. These people always need therapies in order to remain workable or to be able to care for themselves. Stationary rehabilitation and rehabilitation are essential for these patients after acute care. The follow-up therapies on the ground, in the living area, are equally important, but there is a lack of the necessary capacities and possibilities. Patients are neglected, left alone. Their state of health worsens, they can no longer pursue their work and then become unemployed. We should try to prevent this by all means. Unfortunately, in the past, one has failed. These people often fall into depression, and psychosomatic diseases are associated with it, apart from the following diseases caused by the accident. Disability and pain symptoms must be treated repeatedly and subjected to permanent therapy. It is not reasonable for patients to travel 100 kilometres and even further to ergotherapy, physical therapy or logo therapy. There are other injustices. In this context, as an example, I call the two-class medicine. In the periphery, patients do not have the opportunity to get the therapy they would need, but probably in the agglomerations. This abuse is the legacy of a socialist health policy! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] [[Abg. Binder: They were already health speakers in 1996!]] We also know that there is a different supply of victims including rehabilitation measures. In the case of accidents at leisure, in the event of accidents in the household, the corresponding measures are far from as correct and not as feasible as in the case of accidents at work. Thus, in the event of accidents at work, the workers concerned receive the best medical care. In the case of the so-called normal accident victims, rehabilitation is not provided to the necessary extent. The person concerned doesn't get a pension. The consequence is an incapacity or invalidity or disability pension. We must ensure that on-site, residential, group practices, day-care clinics or social services are provided with the possibility of treating patients with rehabilitation measures for long-term therapy, thus ensuring a genuinely continuous medical treatment. It is thus cheaper, more qualitatively equivalent, but above all much more human. Man always has to be at the centre! - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00058_d7e21369	Mr President. High house! With regard to Mr Heinisch's request, which has just been discussed or debated, we can point out that we are very ready to speak and that we will also be dealing with this in the Committee on the Rules of Procedure. As soon as possible – because we have agreed to hold only one debate on this and the following request: Every year, or every six years, the application comes back to the public prosecutor's office. It is true that we ourselves are also considering introducing the right to apply. There will be talks going on. You can say that. With regard to the right of nomination, the reasoning is of course interesting or very treacherous. There it says: "The current regulation is "for the following reasons democratic political concern" - in the opinion of the Greens: 1. For example, if the fourth and fifth strongest parties in the National Council jointly have more mandates than the third strongest party, yet the right of nomination comes to the third strongest party – even if the fourth and fifth strongest parties jointly present a proposal of election, which is then carried by a larger number of delegates." That would have been the copy of an old justification. It would also be possible that the fourth- and fifth-strengths would be stronger together than the second-strength party. However, you always focus on one. It will also be discussed in the Committee on the Rules of Procedure. I can only assure you here and today that neither the Greens nor the Liberals, together with the Liberals, will ever replace the ÖVP as the third strongest force in this House.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00058_d7e21414	Next speaker is Mrs Gatterer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00058_d7e21435	Mr President. High house! In addition to popular desire, referendums and popular surveys, petitions and citizens' initiatives are important instruments of direct democracy. The ÖVP has always been committed to strengthening these direct democratic institutions. I would like to say on behalf of the ÖVP – the colleague of the SPÖ has already mentioned that many of these points are included in the coalition agreement – that the ÖVP has submitted a joint request for the extension of democratic instruments on 1 January. In the form of an own-initiative proposal containing a democratic package, on March 2, 2000, the Commission adopted a proposal for a Council Directive on the approximation of the laws of the Member States relating to the Among other things, this includes the introduction of the election of letters, and a strengthening of direct democracy in the narrower sense and improvements in petitions and citizens' initiatives. We believe that this package should be seen as a whole and negotiated. We have a deadline until 30. It was set and hope that you too will support these essential proposals so that we can implement them. With regard to the development of direct democracy in the narrower sense, it is proposed that popular desires supported by more than 15 percent of those entitled to vote should be subject to mandatory referendums under certain conditions. The petitions and citizens' initiatives are stimulated – and we have already said this in some debates in the Committee – that petitions should not be lost, but that they should be continued in the case of new elections. We also said in the last debate on the collective report that 16 years would be fine for us. We should negotiate all this in one. In the last debate on the assembly report in plenary, we also said that we need to consider how we can achieve improvements in the Committee on Petitions and Citizens' Initiatives in order to provide the citizens with more services and incentives to contact Parliament.
